## 2026-10-31 — HOME candidate recognition and pre-dusk agenda (read-only first; server return NOT accepted)

- User requests that Alice recognize the existing room with bed and wooden door as a potential shelter, know the entrance, leave by door in daytime, and return before dark when in autonomous survival mode. Existing `escape_survey`, `escape_door_action`, `escape_crossing` and `night_shelter` already contain individually bounded door, passage and natural-ground one-block construction primitives, but never proved a safe round-trip route.

Results are `observed_indoor_home_candidate` vs `unverified`. Even a passed geometry scan is NOT monster-proof shelter nor evidence a door opens or an exit/return path succeeds. A 0600 `home-base.json` candidate is saved ONLY when that same bed already has server-confirmed PERSONAL spawn via `HomeBedMemory`. Without a confirmed spawn, an observed room is kept as transient survey rather than fabricated HOME.

For `agentMode=autonomous`, a late-day threshold tick>=8500 can set objective `return_home` if Alice is away from an observed candidate and has no overriding human order/danger; it prevents STARTING ANOTHER distant resource excursion if no safe return route exists. In companion mode no autonomous return overrides the player's commands. `returnRouteVerified:false` is ALWAYS explicit: the planner does not fabricate navigation, request BDS teleports or bypass the independent 2 samples/4s/0.75m gate.

Do not prematurely assert home return completed or shelter construction verified.

## 2026-10-23 — 修复新版 3D 鼠标旋转/缩放手感退化

- 证据：半径 15 格更新将 `fitCamera(... angle,pitch,center,zoom)` 放在逐帧渲染内。用户拖动鼠标时 `angle/pitch` 变化使 camera scale 与 offset 同时重新计算，导致画面发生非主动缩放/平移，偏离此前“围绕 Alice 旋转”的体验；密集地形固定 12FPS 同样增加拖动视觉延迟。
- 修改：保留之前的水平拖动增量 `0.008 rad/px`、垂直 `0.005 rad/px`、俯仰限制 `.24–1.3`、滚轮因子 `.88/1.12`，不引入新交互语义。改为 `fitOrbitCamera`：基于 31×31×8 地形的保守解析包围盒，只依赖画布尺寸/观察半径/主动 zoom，不依赖 angle/pitch 和 Alice 坐标，绘制中心固定，旋转不会自动改变缩放/平移；zoom=1 时角度与俯仰全范围均在画布内。鼠标拖动采用 ~30FPS 和 next-frame 即时刷新；停止交互后仍遵循原先密集 12FPS 节流以减少持续 CPU。
- 仍使用被动区块快照，没有改变 BDS 真实移动坐标、AI 寻路或世界状态；该变更**只涉及 Mac 控制台静态前端**，无需重启 Alice/BDS。回归用例覆盖 4 个视口、6 种水平角度、4 种俯仰角度的全部 31×31×8 顶点，以及滚轮缩放不变焦点。
- 验收：`node --test games/minecraft-ai/bedrock-native/experiment/test/world_camera.test.cjs` 和 Mac 控制台回归；Mac 拉取新 main 并强制刷新 `http://127.0.0.1:8788/#world` 即可，不需要 Mint sync/deploy。

## 2026-10-23 — Evidence-backed death learning, duration telemetry and 3D controls (STAGED, not Mint accepted)

- **Before this change:** Alice persisted total deaths (`life.json`), verified item-skill outcomes, failed work-site cool-downs and explored endpoints, but **not** an attributed cause-of-death learning loop. The mere presence of an enemy or `entityHurt` cannot prove who dealt the fatal hit. Previous deaths are not retrospectively labeled as confirmed causes.
- **New:** `src/death_experience.cjs` saves at most 32 observed death contexts to private, owner-only `~/.alice-bedrock-control/death-experience.json`. Labels are only `recent_damage_and_hostile_nearby`, `hostile_nearby_unconfirmed`, `hunger_exhaustion_possible` or `cause_unknown`. `verifiedCause=false` is mandatory. No player IDs/coordinates, login data, packet payloads, freeform text, arbitrary exception details or guessed killer are stored. Symlinks/unsafe permissions are refused.
- **Applied learning:** >=2 observed deaths with the same nearby *mob type* inside 6h activates `prefer_verified_retreat_over_unconfirmed_attack` for that type. It changes the existing safety controller's decision to attempt a **collision/BDS-verified retreat first**; when no verified route exists, the existing safe last-stand rule remains and no path safety/BDS gate is weakened. Count decays outside the 6h evidence window. This is a bounded reactive policy, **not** proven reinforcement learning, not automatic invulnerability and not an unrestricted Ollama-generated action. Ollama's allowlisted high-level goal request receives an anonymous death summary as advisory context.
- **Timing:** regular async jobs now log `[Alice job] duration:` for completion, exception and cancellation, with `durationMs`, type, source classification and outcome. `ModelGoalScheduler` logs `[Alice agent] planning duration:` and captures wall-clock `planningLatencyMs`; this measures end-to-end model request time, not hidden token-by-token internal inference. 3D HUD reports active job age and the last recent Ollama request duration. Existing 300ms safety tick polling does not create one log per poll.
- **3D/UI:** camera instruction overlay removed; passive sample radius 5→7 (15×15×8 observed voxels), no forced chunks; inventory/drop entity names are shown only when adapter supplies item data; unknown stays unknown. HUD shows real Alice health/food (20-point indicators), emoji behavior and game-derived sun/weather. Side panel shows cautious death-learning conclusions; offline data clears. Nginx permits Bedrock acceptance ZIP export only from Mac loopback via the existing privacy-filtered `export-alice-acceptance.py`.
- **Field acceptance required:** run Mac Python dashboard tests, Mint Node24 full `npm test` and `production:check` through canonical `alice-mac.sh sync` without a restart; only then request a controlled `deploy` to load Node changes. Existing BDS coordinate-witness service was manually disabled by the operator and this code **does not** silently reactivate it. Thus, real navigation may fail closed until a separate independently verified, non-console-spamming witness path exists. Never claim successful AI learning or escape on source tests alone.

Unknown terrain is never converted into safe air. Item entities render an actual adapter-observed item name if present; otherwise display “未知掉落物”, with no guessed item ID.

## 2026-10-23 — New PID 3125378 authenticated/spawned, then died fighting spider; BDS-ready respawn ordering defect (STAGED)

User supplied **current new-PID operator log** `/home/tedz/.alice-bedrock-control/operator-alice-20261023T054159-4b8a12.log`, timestamps 05:42:01–05:45:11 +08:00. This logs a genuine new authenticated Bedrock 2193 session: `login_success` 05:42:04, `start_game_received` 05:42:05, spawn with health restored `respawn_verified` at 05:42:15. Previous 3032s `respawn_stalled` status belonged to OLD PID 3045722 and was a separate shared-status provenance race addressed in `main@9ee559f`.

New Alice chose `LEARN_WEAPON/ACQUIRE_WOOD` at 05:42:20, found natural tree 05:42:25, but Gather stage `pathfinder` failed `unclassified_job_exception` after 5011ms at 05:42:31. It observed 49–65 candidate reachable voxels, attempted exploration at 05:42:45, aborted A* due client↔BDS divergence **0.9m**, then at 05:42:51 local estimate 1.34m without matching authoritative server endpoint (`scout_server_endpoint_unconfirmed`). No evidence that wood was successfully gathered. 05:44:19 spider at 3.4m → ATTACK with unauthenticated `entityHurt`; after taking damage Alice chose EVADE_COUNTER/RETREAT, but guard reported no safe flat floor or **15 routes, zero strictly-away routes**. At 05:44:33 health 0 and new death was independently observed.

**Critical new proof:** on the very new process, retry was issued 05:44:45.575 with `phase=respawn_handshake_retried_once` (both outgoing `respawn state=2` and `player_action` queued back-to-back), then BDS `ReadyToSpawn` arrives **82 ms later at 05:44:45.657**, no subsequent `spawn`/health event; by 05:45:03 `respawn_stalled` with `reason=server_ready_without_spawn`. The original `Pix3lPirat3/mineflayer@b24015a` `lib/bedrock_plugins/health.js` sets `sentRespawnReady` and `sentRespawnAction` flags and suppresses additional final actions after the earlier 5s fallback; therefore the late incoming Ready does not cause that plugin to retransmit final action. A second immediate pre-Ready action from our bounded retry may also be premature. The missing **post-Ready final action** is a directly supported protocol sequence gap; server acceptance still requires BDS acceptance testing, not infer from queue success.

**Fix STAGED only (not deployed):** When dead at 12s and no Ready observed, send exactly one additional `respawn state=2` but DO NOT queue `player_action` yet. When BDS replies with state=1 and Alice is still dead/on same transport, enqueue one (and only one) `player_action respawn` **AFTER the BDS ready**; subsequent duplicate state1 packets cannot requeue. If Ready had already arrived before 12s, keep the existing action-only retry. Still preserve 30s `respawn_stalled`, cancellation on positive-health+spawn or transport close, no new Xbox session, no teleport/world modification. Test assertions cover 12 strict state transitions/order cases in fake EventEmitter; 12/12 pass isolated V8. The actual Bedrock server must still verify new spawn and positive HP. This does NOT prove root cause is resolved until next natural death/respawn.

**Bounded last stand:** When retreat planner refuses ALL observed flat/away corridors and `attempted=false`, Alice now permits a rate-limited melee defense only against a *still-present* hostile within 3.5m, health >0, ready and not a creeper; never substitutes a fake walking route, chases an enemy or claims verified damage. If retreat attempted anything, or mob left reach, or attack cooldown/backoff active, no defensive hit. Readiness/hostile / attack evidence and safety cooldown preserved. Two new Runtime tests cover spider last stand and creeper/attempted/mob-distant refusal, requiring real Mint Node24 regression before promotion.

No file/code in the current PID is hot-reloaded. User can first `alice-mac.sh sync` for full tests without restart; production changes require canonical checked `deploy`. Do not claim remote Alice is offline merely from earlier mixed status, do not repeat destructive restarts.

## 2026-10-23 — Operator status had a stale-heartbeat process identity race (STAGED)

After user ran successful canonical deploy: `npm test: 580/580 PASS`, old PID `3045722` replaced with new `3125378` at 05:41:59. Startup log `operator-alice-20261023T054159-4b8a12.log` had only **2105 bytes** at first query; operator `status` reported `processAlive=true`, `sessionEstablished=false`, `sessionExit=null` but also `deathRecovery.phase=respawn_stalled`, `deadSeconds=3032`, `cycles=3`, `bdsSamples=1273`, travel 54. Contradiction reveals **not a new respawn failure** but an earlier PID's shared `~/.alice-bedrock-control/status.json` being shown under the new owner PID. Old status was written shortly before old process SIGINT, and mtime less than 15 seconds previously was incorrectly counted as a new process's fresh heartbeat. `alice-mint.py status()` only compared st_mtime with now and never checked producing process identity. `diagnose` correctly reported a single new PID, no duplicates. The latest lifecycle summary contained only old PID 3045722 events, ending in legitimate operator SIGINT/shutdown at 05:41:59; this does not prove current PID 3125378 logged in, failed, or disconnected.

**Fix:** every new native production status now carries `producerPid=process.pid` and `producerStartToken` from Linux `/proc/self/stat` start tick (same field operator `inspect(owner)` already verifies). Mac/Mint `alice-mint.py status` refuses inherited or unattributed status unless both PID and birth tick exactly match the current exclusive lock owner, including detection of recycled PIDs. Until the first same-process heartbeat it returns `statusSource=awaiting_current_process_heartbeat`, `statusFilePid` with old known PID or null, `heartbeatFresh=false`, and **null health/deathRecovery/connection/client&BDS positions**—no old death/position/state are attributed to new Alice. `statusSource=current_process` re-enables familiar fields as soon as a real native heartbeat is received. Existing 4KiB compactStatus preserves both essential producer fields. No login, BDS protocol, world, auth, movement, model behavior, or operator stop/start mechanics changed.

Three explicit new Python operator tests: previous-process stale death (current PID different), reused PID with different birth token, and true matching heartbeat. CJS source syntax PASS and read-only source contract verification; **Mint full operator & Node24 tests and actual new login still pending**. The old PID 3125378 won't gain producer fields until future safe `deploy`. To inspect the **current run without restarting**, use the private file (user-supplied) `/home/tedz/.alice-bedrock-control/operator-alice-20261023T054159-4b8a12.log` and compare newest `start_game`/spawn/close lines or `alice-mint.py diagnose`. Never reuse an old session's 3032-second timeout as evidence of current new login failure.

## 2026-10-23 — Bedrock tactical retreat improvements

- The 300ms safety loop no longer uses `lastCombatAt<1250` as a blanket **retreat** blocker: recent melee attack cooldown now throttles only ATTACK. New urgent creeper detection may schedule a guarded retreat even during the earlier attack cooldown, while its own `lastRetreatAt` 4500ms rate limit remains. Within 6.5s of observed self-damage, a threat outside melee reach continues to trigger retreat rather than attempting pointless out-of-range attacks. Per-target `[Alice safety] combat decision:` is deduplicated at most every 10s on unchanged type/tactic and records only sanitized hostile type, tactic, health, rounded distance and recentHit boolean. Source work based on current GitHub `main=769707f` with per-line logs and `diagnose`; preserve the Codex-maintained `scripts/alice-mac.sh` and `scripts/alice-mint.py` entrypoints (no new flags, commands, environment or config keys).

Root causes verified in canonical source: `retreat_routes.cjs` formerly accepted only collision-verified same-Y WALK routes with at least **three steps** and `gain>=1.25m`. The shared `voxel_actions.cjs` flat route catalog omitted **one-step** routes entirely, so an observed one-step escape in a narrow corridor produced no viable route. `runtime.noteHealth()` did not record last self-damage time, and `safety_policy.decideCombatTactic` was called without `lastDamageMs`; the shared `EVADE_COUNTER` path therefore never executed its dedicated emergency retreat. Separately, `operations.retreatFrom()` returned unverified on every move and runtime printed 'remained in place' even when the client had moved.

Implemented without touching raw PlayerAuthInput, native login, Xbox auth, BDS/world/backup, protected navigation or 300ms/5s controller intervals:
- `surveyVoxelActions({includeSingleStep:true})` is **opt-in for retreat only**. Its existing complete 0.6-block collision footprint, headroom, solid-floor, hazard, unknown-block and whole-cell corridor checks are unchanged; ordinary autonomous SCOUT/night routing still excludes one-step routes.
- `planSafeRetreat` accepts 1-step gain >=0.35m, 2-step >=0.65m, 3+ step >=1.15m, max four validated cells, all WALK, monotonically non-approaching a known enemy. Still refuses blind cliff, jumps, mining, doors, unknown chunk, wall or unsupported floor. Goal is **first adjacent** verified waypoint, bounded by existing Pathfinder/3-second timeout.
- `runtime.noteHealth()` records an observed positive self-health decrement; `threat()` now feeds `lastSelfDamageAt` to existing shared threat policy (self-hit exposes nearby hostiles within 10 blocks for 6.5s), and `decideCombatTactic` receives actual `lastDamageMs`. `EVADE_COUNTER` is routed to the retreat branch rather than default attack.
- `BedrockOperations` accepts optional read-only `movementEvidence`. One step is only `serverMovementVerified=true` if a post-checkpoint authoritative server-origin position change >=0.25m is **fresh (<4s)**, increases distance from the selected enemy by >=0.2m and stays aligned with local position within 0.85m. No witness or stale/mismatched/toward-enemy correction → `ok=false`. Logs now distinguish `attempted/localMovedBlocks/serverMovementVerified` rather than always declaring stationary. An event with `entityHurt` remains unattributed and `attackOnce` does not claim server-verified kills.

Offline proof: exact latest `voxel_actions.test.cjs` + `retreat_routes.test.cjs` sources run under isolated V8 Node-compatible assertion harness **21/21 PASS**, including new single-step safe escape, unknown hazard, blocked wall, moving closer; independently exercise actual `BedrockOperations.retreatFrom()` with fake BDS witnesses: success only for fresh away-aligned witness, rejection for mismatched, stale or absent proof. Added four native Runtime regression tests for recent-hit threat/evade and server-authoritative retreat proof. These still require real Mint Node24 full suite (`alice-mac.sh sync` then `alice-mint.py test`) and natural Bedrock survival, no live BDS action, no direct process access, no claim the agent survived a creeper.

Remaining P0 limitations: if even one loaded flat cell does not exist, Alice must refuse blind movement rather than fabricate safe escape; runtime has no guaranteed fallback against creeper; attack damage attribution and actual kill verification remain unproven, low-HP with no observed enemy still cannot invent an escape route. ## 2026-10-23 — Bedrock first-moving-burst post-serialization probe (staged, P0 open)

New optional, owner-only encoded `player_auth_input` roundtrip probe under the existing `ALICE_BEDROCK_INPUT_AUDIT=1` flag. ## 2026-10-22 — RakNet probe not attached at startup: delayedInit connection race identified

Operator's first status after restarting single Alice with ALICE_BEDROCK_INPUT_AUDIT=1: private heartbeat updated, statusCompacted absent, outboundInput present, start_game current_tick not yet observed, queued=0, serialized=0, transportHookAttached=false, transportCalls=0. This startup instant is not evidence send loop failed; the previous subsequent live run had 8,596 queued inputs. Identified actual initialization ordering in pinned bedrock-protocol 3.60.1: createClient({delayedInit:true}) returns a Client with no connection yet; asynchronous discovery/start calls Client.init(), where client.connection becomes a RakNativeClient; connect_allowed is emitted only then. Our opt-in outbound audit attempted to wrap client.connection.sendReliable once on first install, so its transport hook was absent if installed before delayed init. Fix: optional hook now retries only on connect_allowed, start_game and read-only status get(). It preserves original arguments/receiver/results/errors, catches non-writable native methods and exposes transportHookState (connection_pending, attached, method_unavailable, not_writable). This does NOT fix Bedrock movement; it fixes misleading instrumentation.

Another failure mode: the ordinary live status has a strict 4 KiB limit and can drop optional outboundInput after compaction. New src/outbound_input_status.cjs emits a separate, allowlisted, owner-only mode-0600 atomic ~/.alice-bedrock-control/outbound-input-audit.json under the existing root, only when audit is enabled. Includes stage counters, not raw packets, auth or coordinates. Existing status and BDS witness are unchanged. Added 3 delayed init tests and 4 private snapshot tests; original 10 audit tests plus new 3 passed in isolated V8 (13/13). Snapshot tests and entire Mint Node suite remain pending. Requires deploy three source files / two tests and controlled restart of Alice only to apply. No BDS restart, world edit, forced movement, auth reset or guessing packet fields. Subsequent diagnostics must confirm transportHookState=attached, serialized rises over time, and generic transportCalls rises. These are NOT proof a specific move was delivered or accepted; only fresh BDS querytarget delta is authoritative.

## 2026-10-21 — P1 nighttime survival: shelter first, no automatic wait until daylight (staged)

## 2026-10-21 — Continuous autonomy goal lifecycle (staged; Mint Node24/BDS pending)

User evidence from latest BDS after `311dbaad`: with `loginFeet=(0.5,69,0.5)`, two `GATHER` attempts threw `unclassified_job_exception`; the recorded short-term Core state returned `GATHER→IDLE`. Alice then chose `DISCOVER_RESOURCES`, generated a verified-geometry one-block rise to (2,71,-2), proposed `observe` via Ollama, and actually attempted one EXPLORE segment. It returned `scout_server_endpoint_unconfirmed`, `localEstimateBlocks=1.41`, `serverEvidenceReason=no_two_server_positions`, so Core returned `EXPLORE→IDLE`. No server-confirmed travel, resources or jump can be claimed. The 2 attempts had different wood memory sites `0,8,0` and `0,8,-1`, highlighting coordinate-cell boundaries (separate planned issue).

Root architecture problem: `AliceCore` represents current physical task, with persistent human `intent` normally `IDLE`. The `startJob().finally()` calls `core.resumeIntent('job finished')`, legitimately making the action state IDLE; however, objective tracking was only `SelfDriveJournal`/a fresh `decideIdleWork()` at eligible 120s cycles. A completed primitive did not promptly advance the next resource-dependent step. Granite's `ModelGoalScheduler` proposes only one allowlisted operation and remains limited to >=90s between calls, <=12/hour, so it is not an end-to-end action engine.

Implementation is a **goal-continuity foundation**, not an unbounded agent rewrite. New `src/continuous_goal.cjs` separately tracks survival objective `LEARN_WEAPON` / `COLLECT_WOOD` / `OBSERVE`, child step `ACQUIRE_WOOD` / `DISCOVER_RESOURCES` / `CRAFT_WEAPON` / `OBSERVE_WORLD`, and status `READY/ACTING/BLOCKED/WAITING/PAUSED`. Goals survive Core `IDLE`, short-job success/exception, blocked exploration and temporary safety interrupts. Verified inventory changes advance a step; an actual new weapon in the inventory completes the prior `LEARN_WEAPON` objective, NOT a claimed craft response alone. `EXPLORE` with only 1.41 local blocks is blocked as `server_position_unconfirmed`, not successful movement. STOP and FOLLOW pause goal execution; safety preemption also pauses, resuming only after existing safety/health/user gates permit a fresh idle review. Restart persistence stores **only** bounded goal name/version/creation, in private mode-0600 atomic `~/.alice-bedrock-control/continuous-goal.json`, never route, success, position, player name, inventory or model token. Restart treats goal as `REVALIDATE`; fresh inventory and safety gates must decide all next actions. Corrupt/symlinked state is ignored safely.

`runtime.cjs` reconciles a goal from *fresh synchronized inventory* each 5-second eligible idle tick before old 120-second action cadence, updates it when an owned GATHER/CRAFT/EXPLORE/RECOVER begins and ends, and records blocked/unknown outcome without dropping the long-lived objective on IDLE. Crucial functional improvement: an **inventory-delta-verified** autonomous GATHER or CRAFT schedules a goal reassessment in ~5s, rather than the previous generic 120s wait; no accelerated re-attempt after unverified results. Existing `startJob`, `goalPlanner` allowlist/rate caps, STOP/FOLLOW, 300ms Safety, night/health, protected blocks, pathfinder and Bedrock movement-authority boundaries still own every actual physical action. No AI-generated JS, direct shell, unsafe jump or world edit was introduced. The goal controller is a state/action-outcome coordinator, not independent permission to move or mine.

`main.cjs` publishes `longTermGoal` in the private 4 KiB live status; `live_control.cjs` compacts verbose goal metadata while keeping goal/step/phase; `dashboard/app.js` exposes independent current Core action and ongoing objective; private acceptance sample and `[Alice goal] transition:` event retain only allowlisted anonymous statuses/reasons. `dashboard/catalog.json` correctly marks this as staged, not verified gameplay.

Validation performed during development: 5 new continuous-goal unit tests passed in isolated V8 JS including restart/symlink simulation; production `idleTick + startJob/finish` with mocked dependencies verified `LEARN_WEAPON` persists through failed GATHER when Core returns IDLE and moves to CRAFT_WEAPON after real inventory observation, then only ends once a sword is actually in inventory; actual runtime's verified-delta scheduling was tested for 5s continuation versus unverified no-acceleration; JSONL privacy of durable goal events and samples passed. **Mint Node24 `npm test`, complete native BDS running and real inventory/position/jump outcome remain pending.** Previously observed `unclassified_job_exception` cause, no-two-server-positions issue, wood-site boundary and unverified 1-block jump remain separate issues.

## 2026-10-21 — Hotfix: three Mint Node24 sleep-deadline test failures

The user's real Mint `npm test` run reported three failures in `test/runtime.test.cjs` (at L304, L321 and L2018): expected `nextSleepAt=190000` or `130000`, actual `0`. Root cause is definitively in our previous sleep recovery patch: `nextSleepAt` is initialized to `0`, but the failure handler erroneously applied `Math.min(this.nextSleepAt,this.clock()+30000/90000)`. Therefore `Math.min(0,190000)=0`: the requested 30/90s cooldown never scheduled. This is an Alice bug, **not** Node24, network, Bedrock or a test expectation problem.

Fix only that deadline assignment in `src/runtime.cjs`: `this.nextSleepAt=this.clock()+(result?.bedPosition?30000:90000)`. There can be only one active `RECOVER` job; it owns the next bedtime review deadline. This retains individual bed cooldown, separate world-night gate, safety, human FOLLOW/STOP overrides, and does not bypass server sleeping-state confirmation. Existing three Mint failures already exercise this branch; additional regression tests verify initial zero, existing future deadline, both failure types, and success leaves its previous deadline unchanged. Actual patched `startJob` was exercised in isolated V8 with 4 cases and correct numerical values: 190000/130000/130000/unchanged. Full Mint Node24 `npm test` **still must be rerun** before restarting Alice. No gameplay outcome is claimed.

## 2026-10-21 — Bedrock FOLLOW stuck ~4.15 blocks outside range; premature Pathfinder success and invisible stall (staged)

**Fresh user evidence**: after BDS teleport to (0.5,69,0.5), a subsequent Alice restart returned the exact same server `start_game.player_position` without rollback. This establishes BDS saved that **server-authorized teleport**; it does not independently prove acceptance/persistence of Alice's own player-auth-input walking. On command FOLLOW HedyCommon, Mineflayer local observations initially moved 2.83/3.61/8.66 blocks, then >10 consecutive 6.5s samples yielded `localMovedBlocks:0`, `localDistanceToTarget:4.15`, `withinFollowRadius:false`, `pathStatus:success`, `pathMoving:false`, `stalledWindows:0`. The user also observed difficulty climbing a one-block step.

Two **source-confirmed** defects compound:
1. `src/follow_progress.cjs` required distance greater than `safeRadius + 2` before counting a stall. With default radius 3, the 4.15-block standoff could **never** accumulate stalled windows, disabling bounded repath/hold even when no Pathfinder path was moving.
2. Pinned `Pix3lPirat3/mineflayer-pathfinder#789d9146` `GoalFollow.isEnd` floors both target and bot position, and accepts nodes with integer-grid distance <= radius. E.g. target actual X=4.65, bot path node x=1: grid difference 3 => `isEnd:true` even though center-to-target distance is 3.15 and true player-to-target distance may be further. Its `hasChanged` refresh threshold also failed to update until entity moved several cells. The result is the exact `success`, `isMoving:false`, actual out-of-range combination.

Fix in this repository (without editing pinned forks or Bedrock packets): `createResponsiveFollowGoal` overrides only `isEnd` to calculate actual target feet vs candidate node center at a radius 0.55 blocks more conservative than requested. Refresh on each full target-cell movement (not every tiny position jitter). `FollowProgress` counts a stalled attempt whenever outside safeRadius+0.2 and progress is absent, including false `success` with inactive path; two windows trigger bounded non-destructive re-path, then 20s hold after existing bounded repetitions. Already-within-radius player remains stable; STOP/FOLLOW/health/threat gates unchanged.

**Jumping**: pinned pathfinder's `getMoveJumpUp` and `physics.canWalkJump` already use `setControlState('jump',true)`; pinned Bedrock `client_input.js` maps that to `player_auth_input` `jumping`, `want_up`, `start_jumping` intent and simulates jump physics. The supplied logs prove no actual jump or server-accepted ascent; there is no timestamped control-intent evidence. New `follow_input_observation.cjs` passively samples the existing `physicsTick` owner lane while in FOLLOW, exposing `jumpEdges`, `jumpPressedTicks`, `forwardPressedTicks`, `groundedTicks`, `physicsSamples` per follow-progress window. It neither touches Minecraft protocol nor bypasses path collision; *control observations are not outbound packet/BDS movement proof*. On the next live sample: `jumpEdges=0` with a 1-block rise points to planner failing to issue jump; `jumpEdges>0` but no vertical movement indicates client physics, collision or server accept needs further investigation. Still verify BDS `querytarget @a` and another player's game view.

Tests: 15 FollowProgress/GoalFollow/input-observation standalone V8 regressions passed incl 4.15-block freeze, healthy radius, stale integer-grid success, intent counters; full Mint Node24 `npm test` and live BDS climb/heartbeat acceptance remain pending. A separate Alice runtime regression was added. This is a **staged** repair; do not claim one-block jump has been proven in-game. Keep the world backup gate and data untouched.

## 2026-10-21 — Bedrock autonomous bedtime: failed first bed blocks entire night (staged source; real BDS unverified)

New live Mint console evidence: after `[Alice core] IDLE -> RECOVER`, nearby bed `(-1258,99,651)` was selected, nav ran, then `[Alice Bedrock] verified job result: {ok:false,reason:"bed_sleep_rejected:bot did not sleep (not night, monsters nearby, or bed obstructed?)"}`. Alice physically appeared nearby at `(-1259,99,652)` and was in the `night` phase afterwards. This is a **failed or missing sleep-state acknowledgement**, NOT evidence that she never tried, nor proof that BDS specifically refused due to monsters or overhead. The pinned Mineflayer Bedrock `bed.js` calls `activateBlock(click_block)` and waits for `sleep` sourced from server `player_flags` sleeping bit; the timeout lists possible causes but does not identify one.

Root-cause bug in Alice's *recovery policy*: `startJob` always persisted a `sleep:site` 12-minute penalty **in addition** to `bed:x,y,z` 20-minute penalty after one specific bed failed. One Minecraft night is shorter than these blind pauses, so Ollama's separate day-only `LEARN_WEAPON` strategy continues to say `daylight_required` while the real bedtime controller is suppressed. The logs' `RECOVER -> IDLE` only means sleep JOB finished; `result.ok=false` means not confirmed asleep.

Fix:
- Survey nearby beds (up to 24 candidates in radius 12) with sanitized reason categories: safe candidate, no loaded bed, unknown metadata, occupied, nearby hostile, or individual-bed cooldown. Merge paired head/foot into one physical bed to avoid treating the same mattress as an alternate target. Preserve entity safety, sleep time gate, and no invented empty room.
- For a specific failed bed, retain a bounded **per-bed cooldown**, but schedule another-bed resurvey after 30 seconds instead of setting a 12-minute **site-wide** blackout. If no bed was named, schedule a 90-second recheck. ## 2026-10-21 05:50 — Alice sits idle: evidence-backed P0 diagnosis and false motion failure fix (source staged)

Inspected actual `AliceAI-Acceptance-20261021-055029.zip`: 682 JSONL records covering ~04:50:36–05:50:29 SGT (323 10s samples, 359 events). Samples: 238 IDLE, 84 COMBAT, 1 EXPLORE; 151 day, 143 night, 20 dusk, 7 dawn, 2 unknown. Existing `idleHold`: daylight_required 147; movement_evidence_cooldown 74; all_eligible_flat_routes_near_hostiles 56; other 46. Safety dispatched 72 retreats (41 local-only, 27 no verified away corridor, 4 A* noPath), 51 attack observations with no verified damage, and 22 threat transitions. Model proposed 13 observe and 1 scout_safe; this reflects genuinely blocked executor candidates, not an Ollama crash.

At 04:56:59 Alice's client traversed locally 5.66 blocks, and at 05:42:56 3.03 blocks, each with `serverEvidenceReason=no_two_server_positions`, `serverPackets=0`. `classifyScoutFailure('scout_server_endpoint_unconfirmed')` **incorrectly returned `motion`**, triggering a 5-minute minimum site-wide motion penalty, exponentially up to 15m, after *any* normal PlayerAuthInput movement without server corrections. Mojang Player Movement Overview explicitly says the server sends NO `CorrectPlayerMovePredictionPacket` when client prediction is acceptable. It is wrong to treat missing corrections as evidence of rejected movement, or as accepted movement. The existing 20-minute endpoint attempt history is independently useful.

Fix: `world_routine.classifyScoutFailure` now returns `evidence_gap` for exactly named **local-only, no server-proof** situations; true failures (`scout_waypoint_not_reached`, `direct_flat_no_local_motion`, `scout_timeout`, A* noPath) retain their safety penalties. Existing persisted `motion:site` records whose reason is the disproven inference `scout_server_endpoint_unconfirmed` are **ignored without deleting or overwriting private on-disk evidence**; they no longer inflate later real-failure backoff. The autonomous scout completion path retains unverified status and 20-minute endpoint exclusion, does NOT increment confirmed segment count, and schedules a bounded 90s observation rather than 5–15 minute fake failure. It still obeys loaded collision, STOP/FOLLOW, health, threat, nighttime, inventory and independent BDS proof for world modification. This is a fix to the demonstrated false cooldown, NOT proof that BDS accepts or persists movement.

Reconnect remains independent P0: at 05:30:42, `start_game.player_position` from BDS was `(-1260.84,98,653.62)`, confirming the server supplies the old *login* location after Alice had locally moved. Without a `querytarget @a` sample **while Alice is locally away but still online**, the underlying problem cannot be distinguished between BDS rejecting PlayerAuthInput ticks, client-side simulation diverging, or BDS saving/resetting position on disconnect. Do NOT change packets, auth, world files, or teleport based on this uncertainty.

Additional diagnostics: private `[Alice safety] threat context:` journal now retains only safe mob kind and policy tactic, previously stripped by allowlist, so the 72 retreat episodes can be attributed to creeper/other hostile vs stale entity. Adds 90s observation and endpoint proof gap fields. No real Mint or BDS verification yet. Narrow JS isolated checks passed for the patched completion path, known historical motion memory and real waypoint-fault preservation. Full Node24 suite still required. Do not promote console capability from staged to observed until tests and independent BDS live XYZ.

## 2026-10-21 — Terrain-adaptive autonomous exploration: vertical/soil/bridge (source staged)

User requirement: Alice must learn to deal with raised platforms, pits and obstacles using digging and placement, not only walk flat corridors. Existing Bedrock collision survey already *observed* JUMP_UP and STEP_DOWN but discarded them at the autonomous executor boundary. Wood-only harvesting cannot be used for safe navigation edits. Importantly, 04:32 acceptance ZIP still had zero passive authoritative self-position correction packets, and independent BDS XYZ confirmation was not yet supplied; neither local physics nor Mineflayer return success may authorize destructive world edits.

Implemented:
- `src/voxel_actions.cjs` publishes observed *adjacent* vertical transitions from the same bounded collision geometry scan; `src/terrain_adaptation.cjs` selects one JUMP_UP/STEP_DOWN without unknown support, hazardous blocks, enemies or recently attempted endpoints. Alice can submit **one** height-transition waypoint to the existing cautious pathfinder after independent fresh collision reinspection. Pathfinder dig, parkour, door auto-open, towers remain disabled. `boundedScout` does not use the flat input fallback for vertical failures; travel only confirmed with Bedrock server-position evidence.
- Read-only earthwork classification: a two-block-deep one-cell pit with known solid reference floor and a safe opposite landing may become `PLACE_BRIDGE`; an isolated grass/dirt obstruction with natural ground evidence may become `DIG_SOIL`. Loaded 5x5 floor/body/head neighborhoods are checked for player construction, hazards and unknown blocks. Chests, walls, doors, glass, cobblestone and other built material prohibit alteration. No overhead/foundation excavation, ores, wood, water, lava, sand/gravel gravity, multi-block bridges or pillar builds.
- One bounded `executeEarthwork` operation is allowed **only with two or more fresh server-origin self-position samples matching Alice's current position**, survival/daylight/hostile/FOLLOW/STOP/job-owner checks, re-surveyed identical target, synced known inventory and API availability. At most one selected block change, with a five-minute global attempt interval; failure sets existing route cooldown. Dig is verified only with both observed soil disappearance and authoritative inventory dirt pickup; bridge only when support collision + selected inventory decrease are observed. Success does **not** prove traversal; next exploration must re-survey. If BDS self-position packets remain at 0, mining/placing stays blocked by design.
- Ollama may request only the allowlisted `adapt_terrain` *intent*; it cannot supply coordinates, material, or an arbitrary command. The deterministic executor decides if the proof is strong enough. Runtime and 3D control heartbeat/acceptance journal expose short `terrainAdaptation.vertical`, `terrainAdaptation.earthwork`, `positionProof` with status `staged`.
- Added `test/terrain_adaptation.test.cjs` (11 hazard/provenance/interaction checks) and runtime/model/status regressions. Eleven adaptation tests ran in an isolated JS harness; full native Node24, Mac Python and Minecraft BDS gameplay were **not** executed in this environment. Never represent proposed `PLACE_BRIDGE`, `DIG_SOIL` or locally reached heights as physically confirmed.

Review `AGENTS.md` and world-backup production preflight; deploy with non-destructive Mac rsync, restart *only* Alice once, and independently check Minecraft `querytarget @a` and actual item/block deltas before promoting any action from staged to observed. No dashboard safety override or new arbitrary commands.

## 2026-10-21 — Autonomous progress/memory, retreat corridor, verified wood→sword closure (staged source, pending Mint/BDS)

Source of need: `AliceAI-Acceptance-20261020-234048.zip`, previously analyzed in conversation: 331 status samples + 297 classified events over ~22:40–23:40 SGT; 15 Granite requests all `observe`; 4 bounded EXPLORE tasks none BDS-attested, and repeatedly selected local A/B endpoints around [-1261,98,656] and [-1265,98,652] with local-only 5–6 block motions. 63/68 retreat results yielded `no_verified_safe_tile` under an endpoint-only retreat heuristic. No actual inventory-verified wood pickup or sword crafting was recorded. The client-side pose is NOT reliable independent evidence of server-accepted motion; zero Bedrock correction packets is inconclusive. This task does not redefine that trust boundary.

New `src/exploration_progress.cjs`: bounded private `~/.alice-bedrock-control/exploration-progress.json` (mode 0600; avoids symlink target) stores up to 48 unique endpoint keys, TTL 20 minutes for attempted/blocked/unverified and 60 minutes for *server-confirmed* endpoints. An autonomous EXPLORE job records its candidate endpoint *when it actually starts*; when it finishes, `outcome` remains `unverified` unless both `ok`, matching `confirmedEndpoint` and a real server position delta exist. All recent attempts are excluded from `selectSafeWalkingCandidate` along with existing verified visits and route-specific failures. This breaks repeated attempts to the same A/B local-only endpoints without fabricating confirmed travel; if all routes are excluded Alice stays put and waits for new observation/expiry rather than wandering into unknown space. The new `explorationProgress` summary and `endpoint attempt/feedback` events are published with explicit proof distinction. This memory has bounded lifetime, not a global semantic map or authoritative player location.

New `src/retreat_routes.cjs` uses **the same collision-shape-aware Bedrock voxel scanner** (loaded complete-footprint support and headroom, no unknown blocks, hazardous tiles, jumps or unsupported elevations) to consider multiple fully WALK routes. It selects a corridor that does not approach the observed threat and gains at least 1.25 blocks over an up-to-four-step prefix; physical retreat uses only the **first** confirmed-geometry adjacent cell in A* with dig/parkour/doors disabled, max 3-second deadline. If there is no verifiable corridor, it reports a specific reason instead of guessing three distant endpoint tiles. Even if the local position changes, outcome remains `retreat_local_only_server_unverified`, **never claims BDS-verified escape**. Existing exported `chooseRetreat` API stays location-or-null for old tests. It does NOT solve all safety cases or introduce unsafe native pulses.

New `src/resource_opportunity.cjs`: a bounded read-only three-column/natural-tree survey reuses the existing **strict** `inspectNaturalTree` proof (trunk, canopy, known block data and nearby construction veto). When the existing autonomy policy genuinely needs resources and world is safe daylight (health >=16, no movement-failure cooldown), Alice can start exactly **one** autonomous GATHER even if Granite returns only `observe`. When observed inventory materials suffice, it can start exactly one wooden-sword CRAFT using the existing recipes. Both call `runVerifiedResourceStep`, wrapping the old gather/craft executor and measuring before/after Bedrock inventory counts; *no actual delta means no success*, regardless of a claimed tool return. Failed GATHER uses existing site cooldown, failed CRAFT gets an independent ten-minute cooldown to avoid infinite repeats. New `[Alice mission] resource opportunity`, `inventory milestone`, and `craft deferred` evidence is privacy-filtered; no arbitrary structures may be dug and no client-estimated movement is accepted as item proof.

Tests: new 4 exploration memory tests, 4 retreat corridor tests, 5 resource opportunity tests, 3 runtime integration tests, and 2 JSONL/status telemetry regressions. 9 route/resource unit cases passed in isolated JS V8 harness after correcting an invalid wall-fixture expectation, and 5 memory invariants passed via a mocked private filesystem (including 20-minute expiry and symlink refusal). The modified .cjs JS source parses; **the full Mint Node24 suite, Mac Python suite, Bedrock BDS gameplay, and any actual wood/sword outcomes HAVE NOT been run by assistant**. No world, credentials, backups, BDS state or player-owned structures were modified. These are staged code changes, not in-game completion.

Required next validation after synchronization: run Mac `python3 -m unittest discover -s games/minecraft-ai/tests -v`; rsync changes to Mint without `--delete`; Mint Node24 `node --test test/exploration_progress.test.cjs test/retreat_routes.test.cjs test/resource_opportunity.test.cjs test/runtime.test.cjs test/acceptance_log.test.cjs test/live_control.test.cjs`, then `npm test`, `npm run production:check` and restart only the one Alice process preserving original verified auth/world/backup/Ollama environment. Keep BDS and dashboard server unchanged. On Mac download ZIP with existing `collect-alice-acceptance-mac.sh 1`. Confirm anti-loop candidate exclusions, retreat path results, Bedrock inventory deltas and ideally independent BDS `querytarget @a` before/after movement. Never claim safe exploration or crafted sword without evidence.

## 2026-10-20 — Collision-aware voxel planning M0/M1a staged source

Real first implementation following `docs/ALICE_BLOCK_WORLD_ACTION_PLANNER.md`: `src/voxel_actions.cjs` observes a bounded radius-4 region with <=640 Bedrock `blockAt` reads and <=96 BFS states. Its support/clearance evaluation reads **actual registry collision shapes** instead of excluding floors on the basis of a natural-grass/stone whitelist. It proposes explicit, **not yet executable** WALK, JUMP_UP and STEP_DOWN waypoints, including half-slab height. Unknown blocks/shapes, hazards, unsupported 0.6m player footprint and unverified collision cause rejection. The proposed route never mutates the world.

In `runtime.idleTick` the survey is taken once at an eligible idle cycle, sent as a bounded `voxelNavigation` summary to the existing Ollama planner, and published under `worldRoutine.voxelNavigation` in the 4KiB live status (subject to existing status compaction). Logs `[Alice voxel] collision-aware route observed` on meaningful survey change. Previously `observe` returned with no new world evidence; it now records read-only collision observations and suppresses identical observation-only model calls for 20 min **unless health, inventory, route/voxel evidence, safe daylight or scout availability changes**. This is not a claim of a successful physical action.

A fully **flat WALK-only** corridor of 3–8 verified waypoints can act as a backup `scout_safe` candidate when the natural-only scout route fails, the day/health/wood-failure/movement cooldown guards allow exploration, the target is not a recent failed/visited route, and no known hostile is within 9 blocks of its endpoint. It still passes through the existing bounded Bedrock `boundedScout` action, with no dig/place, no door interaction and mandatory server movement evidence before claiming progress. The new planner does **not** send jump, descending, mining or placing inputs: those have additional execution/physics and building protection requirements.

Seven independent `test/voxel_actions.test.cjs` cases cover built floors, one-block rise, half-slab, hazards, unloaded chunks, protected dig/place non-use, collision uncertainty and bounded reads. A matching local isolated Node22 prototype passed its earlier six behavioral cases; the **final Mint Node24 full suite and real BDS position/physics tests are pending**. No BDS/world/backups/Xbox data changed by this source commit.

## 2026-10-20 — Bedrock player logout cancels stale FOLLOW (implementation staged)

The user reported 15 status samples spanning ~30s with Alice online, IDLE, `followTarget:null`, fixed **local** position -1261.4,99,654.5, `movementEvidence.received:0`. This is consistent with no active FOLLOW but does **not** establish that the correct player-offline signal was received. User requests: if the human *truly disconnects* from BDS, cancel FOLLOW/GUARD; if merely distant/out of loaded entity range, keep the instruction.

Verified the **exact pinned** `Pix3lPirat3/mineflayer@b24015aeeec68a5c94f54444f104f0e563595a30`: Bedrock `lib/bedrock_plugins/entities.js` lines 42–49 / 346–352 removes `bot.players[username]` on `player_list remove` and emits `playerLeft(player)`. A Bedrock `remove_entity` / `change_dimension` removes the entity only, emits `entityGone`, and may leave the player roster intact. This distinguishes **server roster departure** from **range unload** without polling and without guessing logout on missing coordinates.

The Bedrock runtime now subscribes to `playerLeft` while started and detaches on stop. It only revokes a matching active FOLLOW/GUARD owner after confirming no case-insensitive same-name roster entry remains (protect rapid rejoin). It abandons old last-seen coordinates, only stops a FOLLOW-owned path/input, resets durable intent to IDLE and records a bounded `lastFollowEnd` in private live status. It does NOT cancel an unrelated active human task, interfere with SURVIVE/COMBAT escape, drop other players' follow ownership, or interpret `entityGone` as a logout. Rejoined users must explicitly re-command FOLLOW/GUARD; no silent auto-resume. Existing model/idle safety policy continues. No BDS/world/login cache/backup changes.

New tests cover playerLeft routing, unrelated departure, entityGone, rapid rejoin, safety preemption, temporary job preservation, and listener cleanup. **No real Mint tests or Minecraft acceptance has been performed by this source commit**; use Node24 tests, restart only the single Alice bot and test actual join/leave/rejoin while querying local status. ## 2026-10-20 — Ollama MLX HTTP 501 workaround (opt-in, not a model success claim)

User's Mint -> Mac `POST /api/chat` using `gemma4:26b-a4b`, `format:"json"` returned HTTP **501** after ~9 s; therefore no output file was written by `curl -f`. A published Ollama MLX report documents the same HTTP 501 when `libollama_xgrammar.dylib` (structured decoding library) is missing in some Homebrew builds. This remains a **likely diagnosis, not proof** until the user's exact HTTP 501 response body and Ollama packaging/version are checked. The failed call is not a model-inference benchmark.

To support this without loosening Bedrock tool permissions: `OllamaPlanner` now takes `jsonMode:'strict'|'prompt'` (default **strict**), forwarded from Mint env `ALICE_OLLAMA_JSON_MODE`. Opt-in `prompt` omits only the optional Ollama `format:'json'` field when proposing an autonomous goal. The existing exact `JSON.parse` + `validateGoal` one-tool whitelist still rejects prose, extra keys, arbitrary commands, invented coordinates and multi-object responses. `ALICE_OLLAMA_TIMEOUT_MS` allows a deliberately chosen 1000–120000 ms instead of fixed 12000 ms, default unchanged; useful to test cold-loaded MLX but verify measured latency first. `keep_alive:'0'` remains unchanged for Mac resource conservation, and the model remains serialized with human chats. No automatic retries or hidden additional model calls, no BDS restart, no auth/backup/learning state modifications.

Mint diagnostic: test without `format` first, capturing HTTP body and request wall time even for failures. If successful and the JSON passes local validation, configure `ALICE_OLLAMA_JSON_MODE=prompt` plus `ALICE_OLLAMA_MODEL=gemma4:26b-a4b` and a measured `ALICE_OLLAMA_TIMEOUT_MS` (e.g. 60000), then test with Node24. Model status remains `unverified` until a real successful valid proposal. **No user's model request, full test suite, or BDS integration has been re-run by this commit.**

## 2026-10-20 — Live open-door crossing reaches real movement stall; passive collision diagnosis staged

**New Mint real-server evidence:** production Xbox login and spawn succeeded with the session log guard. A fresh indoor read-only survey at center [-1261,98,653] found wooden door [-1261,98,655], complete safe 1-step approach cell [-1261,98,654], no unknown blocks and 18 observed reachable cells. Daytime autonomy selected FIND_ROOM_EXIT/WAIT and started EXPLORE. It issued five bounded forward pulses toward the observed opposite cell [-1261,98,656] and returned `crossing_no_local_forward_progress` (attempted=true, serverMovementVerified=false), then EXPLORE -> IDLE. No automatic digging or damage. This is proof a movement attempt was scheduled, **not** proof that the actual BDS player moved, or of the precise cause of the local stall.

**Pinned adapter facts:** Pix3lPirat3/mineflayer `lib/bedrock_plugins/client_input.js` at b24015ae stores forward control state; its 50ms send timer emits player_auth_input and runs Bedrock-aware prismarine-physics on local world.getBlock -> bot.blockAt. Its own physics world uses block.shapes to determine collisions, so an open door whose block collision geometry does not rotate with open_bit could stop local movement; a stale yaw, missing physics ticks, or server correction would also produce a similar symptom. The session log alone does not distinguish them, therefore do not assume that open_bit=true proves a clear physics corridor and do NOT fake door blocks as air or increase blind movement time.

Added `experiment/src/door_motion_diagnostics.cjs`, a passive bounded probe attached only during a guarded crossing: records physicsTick count, ticks with forward held, horizontal-collision ticks, local starting/ending/maximum displacement, player yaw vs expected door axis, ground/velocity/physicsEnabled/vehicle, observed lower/upper door open and collision shapes, and read-only movementEvidence server packet counts/timestamps/last source. On a `crossing_no_local_forward_progress` or endpoint/corridor failure, it is included in the result and logged once as `[Alice exploration] doorway movement diagnostic:`. No packet wrapping, no credential/entity IDs, no changed server/client protocol or world/block data, no added forward pulses. Listener removed on every success/failure/STOP path. New isolated `door_motion_diagnostics.test.cjs` covers cleanup, collision, server-vs-local distinction and no side effects.

**Validation pending:** source syntax and isolated synthetic stall simulation; full Mac/Mint Node24 npm tests, one controlled Mint live run and BDS `querytarget` comparison. Next decision must be driven by captured `doorInitially.*.shapes`, `forwardPhysicsTicks`, `horizontalCollisionTicks`, yaw and `serverPacketCountDelta`. Continue strict STOP/FOLLOW/hostile/health gates, exclusive Xbox login lock and world backup check. Do not restart BDS or delete auth cache.

## 2026-10-20 — Real Alice still inside: protected-gather failure did not promptly schedule room escape (staged)

Mint production PID 171925 Xbox login/spawn succeeded. Daytime autonomy selected LEARN_WEAPON/WORK, then GATHER scanned 103 log blocks, zero leaves, 59 tree columns: 53 rejected near player structure (planks/furnace) and six lacking trunk evidence. Gather returned 0/1 no_verified_natural_tree, persisted a 10-minute wood-site cooldown, and transitioned GATHER -> IDLE. The supplied log has **no escape survey, approach, door interaction or crossing**. This indicates she had not started the exit process, not that a known door traversal had failed.

**Verified scheduling code defect:** idleTick selects normal autonomous WORK and sets nextIdleAt to a generic 2-minute work cooldown. When the first GATHER fails and marks a resource area blocked, it previously never advances nextIdleAt, so the indoor escape scan under the later WAIT branch could not run until ordinary work was eligible again. New runtime shortens ONLY the next safe idle evaluation to now+5000ms after an unsuccessful autonomous GATHER/CRAFT, preserving the 10-minute resource-site rejection and all STOP/FOLLOW/safety/health invariants. A read-only survey now happens before SCOUT-vs-WAIT choice. An *actionable* loaded door and route can take priority over ordinary daytime natural-ground SCOUT; unknown/unreachable doors cannot block an otherwise safe SCOUT. A prior motion-failure cooldown denies door actions explicitly and cannot be circumvented by the escape branch.

Regression tests added: failed resource harvest promptly schedules indoor survey but does not repeat gather or alter blocks; actionable door beats SCOUT; server-movement uncertainty permits observation but blocks indoor physical actions. ## 2026-10-20 — Alice Bedrock intermittent server_id_conflict: shutdown lifecycle + session guard (staged)

**New live evidence:** Oct 19 MCSManager `bedrock-default` BDS UDP 17879, world `Bedrock level`, continuously-running PID 2875730, server output managed by `pty_linux_x64`. The operator found its console log and correlated two `server_id_conflict` client attempts (22:11:01 and 23:36:54) with the BDS player's disconnect recorded 4–5 seconds *later*. Successful fresh logins were recorded at 22:11:38, 22:58:09, 23:06:16 and 23:18:42. This is consistent with but does not prove a stale server-side session causing the rejection; confirm the game account is the Alice identity before using that player name as a preflight key.

**Confirmed code issue:** pinned Mineflayer `bot.end()` selects `bedrock-protocol.close()` (no protocol disconnect request), and the autonomous launcher immediately called `process.exit(0)`. The legacy-chat launcher similarly forced exit after `disconnect`. Both Bedrock launchers now share `bedrock-native/session_lifecycle.cjs`: bounded idempotent Ctrl+C/SIGTERM handling, stop controllers before explicit `client.disconnect()`, no immediate forced exit, transport-close fallback with an explicit **server logout unverified** warning. An optional, strictly fail-closed **when configured** `server_session_guard.cjs` inspects the explicitly selected recent read-only MCSManager log *before* creating Xbox transport; blocks if the last exact Gamertag event is connected/spawned or unknown. It never reads/authors credentials, modifies BDS, or assumes local close equals remote disconnect. Without both environment variables the protection is **not enabled**, and the launcher prints a warning, retaining original compatibility. More in `docs/BEDROCK_SESSION_LIFECYCLE.md`.

New isolated Node tests cover signal stop, repeated stop, closed transport, failure fallback, recent BDS disconnect, still-online, stale/missing/unreadable, symlink and other-player cases. **Source syntax validated; full Mint Node24 npm test, log permissions, real Bedrock stop/launch, actual server-side logout and reduced server_id_conflict rate remain UNVERIFIED.** No server reboot, Xbox credential deletion, online-mode downgrade, world modification, or repeated native-vs-Mineflayer A/B was done. `MaxListenersExceededWarning` remains a separate unresolved listener issue.

## 2026-10-19 — Mint 3D viewer symlink test regression (fixed source, target retest pending)

- User ran Mint `npm test`: `test/world_view.test.cjs:72` failed because after symlinking private `world-view.json` to a nonexistent destination, `writer.lastError` was `null` instead of `Unsafe viewer target`. This is **not** a failure of the 3D renderer; it exposed a security precondition bug in the private atomic file publisher.
- Root cause: `fs.existsSync(path)` follows symbolic links, returning false for a dangling link, so `existsSync(path) && lstatSync(path).isSymbolicLink()` missed it. Changed `bedrock-native/experiment/src/world_view.cjs` to direct `fs.lstatSync(path,{throwIfNoEntry:false})?.isSymbolicLink()` for **both snapshot file and private root directory**. New Node tests assert a dangling file link remains untouched and that a dangling root-directory link is rejected; normal nonexistent targets and mode-0600 atomic publishing remain expected to work.
- Isolated Node.js 22 reproduction confirmed the old existsSync false negative and new lstat detection for both links; **Mint Node24 test suite after this fix, production:check, and real #world observation are not yet verified**. Keep capability status `staged`. No Alice/world/backup/credential changes or cache clearing. Canonical operator commands and handoff: `docs/ALICE_WORLD_VIEW_3D.md` and `docs/HANDOFF_2026-10-19_WORLD_VIEW.md`.

## 2026-10-19 · Alice 单独在线的整区探索冷却阻塞（已修源码，Mint 待实测）

- **实测根因**：Mint 已加载最新 `scout_routes.cjs` 且 Alice 进程晚于源码更新13秒，但她在两个白天均输出 `FIND_NEW_RESOURCE_AREA / WAIT / scout_site_cooldown`。只读私有失败记忆：`scout:-79,12,40`、`no_verified_safe_corridor`、`attempts=4`、剩余10.7分钟；此外同一张床 `bed:-1258,99,651` 连续两次失败（另一问题）。`world_routine.cjs` 旧区域级 `scout` 缺陷直接在 `candidateScout()` 调用前拦截，导致新3D路线没有机会执行。
- **本轮实现**：`FailureMemory` 原子私有记录保留所有合法旧 `scout` 历史，仅作为 `legacyScoutIgnored` 审计，不再阻断新的三维路线；`route:<x>,<y>,<z>` 保存失败的确切 Pathfinder waypoint（非整个16x8x16区域，也不是任意远处终点），BFS 避开此节点但可尝试其他加载且安全的分支；`probe:<x>,<y>,<z>` 保存未加载/未知或不安全地形的短期观察结果，**不阻止新地形一旦加载就重新规划**；`motion:<site>` 专门冻结真正的服务器/本地位置未确认事件，避免在移动证据不可信时换方向乱走。保持 `wood`、`sleep`、`bed` 既有保护独立有效。动态路线继续只有白天/已加载安全地面/安全生命值可试；禁止挖掘、放置、未知区块跳跃和抢占人类玩家命令。
- 地形扫描诊断已能区分 `current_ground_unloaded`、`current_ground_unverified`、`terrain_unloaded_or_incomplete` 与 `no_verified_3d_surface_route`。旧 `no_verified_safe_corridor` 是历史状态而非新规划器的失败结果。自主 WAIT 时每30秒重新评估观察，真实路线级 `noPath` 可在15秒后根据排除的失败节点重新选候选，`scout_server_endpoint_unconfirmed` 则有专属5～15分钟安全暂停；`scout`、`route`、`probe`、`motion` 均不改变 Xbox 登录/世界/背包数据。
- `dashboard/#control` 显示地形观察、失败路线数量、独立服务器移动暂停和旧记录忽略状态。新增 `test/world_routine.test.cjs`、`test/runtime.test.cjs` 回归，隔离纯路线测试14项通过、私有模拟重启遗留记录/精确route/0600权限检查通过；**Mint 真实 Node24 `npm test` 和 BDS 服务器XYZ运动尚未执行，因此功能仍 staged**。交接以 `docs/HANDOFF_2026-10-19_MOVEMENT.md` 最新章节为准。

> **2026-10-19 认证代码溯源补充**：上游真实固定依赖为 `bedrock-protocol@3.60.1` → `prismarine-auth@^3.1.1`。已查 `prismarine-auth@3.1.1/src/MicrosoftAuthFlow.js`，`getMinecraftBedrockChain(publicKey)` 中 Bedrock 证书链缓存分支以 `&& false` 明确禁用；每次调用向服务端索取绑定本次公钥的证书链，同时重新向 Minecraft 服务索取多人游戏登录令牌。因此不应无证据要求用户删除 Xbox 缓存作为“解决旧证书复用”的措施。现有匿名握手审计增加 `certificatePublicKeyMatches`、`userChainHeaderKeyMatches`（仅布尔/未知，不保存公钥本体）；它们用于区分当前客户端证书绑定不一致，但不是 BDS `server_id_conflict` 的已确认根因。

> **2026-10-19 P0 login hardening (GitHub code stage only):** Read actual pinned bedrock-protocol@3.60.1 `src/client.js`, `src/client/auth.js`, `src/handshake/login.js`: network_settings causes sendLogin; login packet is written immediately before `loggingIn`. First-party protocol enum distinguishes `ServerIdConflict=44` from `LoggedInOtherLocation=43`. Prior native A/B and at least one live `legacy-native-injected` spawn prove that path sometimes works but are NOT a permanent fix. No confirmed server-side rejection reason yet.
>
> Implemented passive `bedrock-native/connection_audit.cjs`: both native chat-only and autonomous Bedrock connectors observe session/login/spawn/disconnect events and store last 32 anonymous outcomes under private `~/.alice-bedrock-control/connection-audit.json` (0600). At `loggingIn`, check *only* invariant booleans/bounds: JWT structure, client/certificate name consistency, DeviceOS/platform consistency, ServerAddress and game version matching, expiry windows and token presence, never output access tokens, XUID, Gamertag, JWT body, DeviceId, SelfSignedId, multiplayer token or Xbox cert. On `server_id_conflict`, compare safe field names to preceding spawn evidence; does not guess exact server root cause. `dashboard/remote_control.py` exposes last 6 strongly typed, allowlisted records over read-only `GET /api/control/history`, shown in Mac console regardless of Alice online status. The old/native and new/autonomous paths also now share `bedrock-native/instance_lock.cjs` for exclusive single-process Xbox login; second launch is refused **before** creating the authenticated transport. A stale PID-owned lock is reclaimed only when owner process is definitively dead and confirmed dead; never kill services or touch world/cache. This eliminates one preventable simultaneous-login pattern **after both entrypoints are upgraded**, but no claim that it solves this intermittent BDS reason.
>
> Rollout not yet executed on Mint; new Node audit/lock tests and Python dashboard tests were added to source. Native/legacy entrypoints require one source deployment, and existing bot must be stopped before updating in-use code, without resetting world, cache or backup. Users were explicitly exhausted by repeated A/B tests: do not request historical native login re-tests. Resume real movement fixes only after login stability is evidence-based.

> **协议边界**：Bedrock `DisconnectFailReason` 44 = `ServerIdConflict`，43 = `LoggedInOtherLocation`；不能从 44 单独断言重复客户端/XUID，不得自行推断 Microsoft 的历史 service-abuse 事件就是此次 44 的原因。服务器身份/会话状态、Xbox 服务响应与登录证书验证链等仍是**未区分的候选**，必须有现成的 BDS 同时刻服务端证据才可定位。下一轮工程应直接读现有服务端登录拒绝日志/现有诊断缓存或完善**被动、脱敏**的关联记录，且要让用户不需额外重做旧版 A/B、重复启停或重新授权；不能自动重试、删除 `~/.alice-bedrock-auth`、重启 MC 世界或禁用 online-mode。

## Evidence from user-run Mint tests

- Alice joined Paper 1.21.11; Floodgate resolves Bedrock player `MolarParsley191` to `.MolarParsley191`.
- Instant Mandarin `跟着我` routed to `!followPlayer` and Alice visibly followed.
- `[Alice movement] ... STUCK ... moving=true` showed pathfinder goal active but lack of physical displacement; user identified blocking terrain.
- Recovery `repath` moved Alice closer in reported runs; learning log recorded 2 successes.
- `FOLLOW -> GUARD -> FOLLOW` observed around combat.
- Wood collection sometimes timed out (`Took to long to decide path to goal!`, `Digging aborted`); one run obtained only 6 logs.
- A previous long-running LLM command could return after a follow instruction and steal movement. Local fixes were discussed/applied interactively; exact Mint file state is not remotely readable here.
- Death interrupted follow and required another instruction, motivating state persistence.
- `自己做工作台，学习如何做武器` triggered long LLM wait before deterministic recipe workflow was designed.
- Prior `AliceLearningSystem` constructor ran before `agent.name` was initialized; initialization order was corrected in Mint testing.

## Delivered here

- Canonical module boundaries and exported interfaces for existing Mint adapter references.
- Safe installer with exact integration preflight, automatic backup, repeatable checksums, drift refusal and rollback.
- Isolated unit tests for installer invariants and JavaScript syntax smoke checks (to run on Linux).
- Documentation for ongoing capability tracking and operational handoff.

## Not yet claimed

- This commit has **not** been installed or field-tested on Mint.
- The existing Mint integrations are not under version control yet; treat installation as a carefully gated transition.
- Full recursive Minecraft planning, food hunting, advanced tool chains, retained FOLLOW over reconnects, environment-aware safe destruction and self-learning of novel skills are not verified.
- Autonomy is deterministic inventory/weapon housekeeping, not unconstrained play.

## Next acceptance run

1. `python3 scripts/install.py --check` must pass against the actual Mint checkout, with no mutation.
2. Back up and install; validate `node main.js` connection.
3. Check follow on flat ground, then step/blocked terrain, then guarding after combat, then death/respawn.
4. `收集10个木头` and `自己做工作台，学习如何做武器`: verify actual inventory increase, not just command output.
5. Leave Alice idle for 20 seconds; verify no autonomous action while FOLLOW intent is active.
6. Capture logs and revise diagnosis rather than stacking unspecified compatibility fallbacks.

## Current implementation: modular deployment and combat safety (2026-10-18)

- Added Mac one-command deployment script and Mint helper. Workflow: Git main ff-only pull, rsync, existing-bot guard, compatibility preflight, Python/Node unit tests, backup/install, foreground startup. These operations **have not been executed across the user's SSH connection**.
- Added `equipment.js`, `safety_policy.js`, `safety.js`, `crafting.js`. Static ECMAScript parse checks passed; 5 pure policy/equipment assertions passed in an isolated evaluator. These do not establish Mineflayer/Paper behavior.
- Old Mindcraft `skills.collectBlock` already uses `bot.tool.equipForBlock`, and `skills.defendSelf` already selects weapons; observed failures are thus primarily **danger scheduling and command preemption**, not proof of absent tool features.
- Runtime now initiates reactive safety with autonomy, even during GATHER/CRAFT. In real Minecraft still verify immediate preemption, correct sword, owner selection via Floodgate name, task resumption, low-HP retreat and protection from concurrent modes.
- Existing Mindcraft integration files remain Mint-local and are guarded by installer preflight; do **not** claim complete end-to-end priority until the action-manager integration is versioned and live tested.

## Phase 0.3: terrain, location and tactical health (2026-10-18)

- Implemented block-based short-range terrain classification; narrow solid lane is permitted, unknown blocks, air gap and hazardous floor block manual jump recovery. Regular Pathfinder is configured with no sprint/parkour and one-block drop limit; it is not yet measured against a real 1-block cliff lane.
- Implemented `AlicePlayerTracker`: visible player entity -> local last-seen waypoint -> optional trusted Paper feed. It cannot infer fresh coordinates once the entity is unloaded and no provider is active. A provider plugin's source and opt-in configuration were added, **not built or installed on the user's Paper server**.
- Implemented health tiers and evade-then-counter tactics, creeper avoidance, and optional food consumption to permit game-mechanic regeneration; not yet observed in real mob fights.
- Added pure regression tests for waypoint freshness/dimension and terrain hazard and combat tactic selection. Node tests require running from the deployed project mirror; full server integration must still be proven.
- The safe Mac one-command update/start procedure remains unchanged and deliberately does not restart Paper or install plugins.


## Preflight regression: optional message-epoch marker (2026-10-18)

Mint's one-command deployment failed **before installation** with:
`src/agent/agent.js is missing integration markers ['_aliceMessageEpoch']`.
The script had already copied the project mirror to `~/minecraft-ai`, but did not replace any managed `~/mindcraft-alice/src/agent/alice_*.js` modules or start Alice. Existing `bots/AliceAI` learning files and server world were untouched by the failed installer.

Root cause: we incorrectly made optional stale-LLM command epoch support a mandatory installation prerequisite. Required Alice Core/action/mode/command adapters are still checked. Missing epoch now produces an explicit warning instead. When no verified epoch exists, interrupted wood work will **not** restart automatically after combat, rather than replay a possibly superseded order. Messages/commands from all players remain permitted by this Alice project; no owner-command authorization filter was introduced. The actual Mint Chat/LLM bridge still needs source review for complete stale-response cancellation.

After pulling this fix, rerun the same Mac one-command deployment. The running-bot guard must still be respected. This change has been source-reviewed and test coverage authored; it has not yet run through the user's actual Mint environment.

## Phase 0.4: read-only status + friendly MC chat (2026-10-18)

- Implemented `AliceSocial` and `AliceActivity` modules and wired them from `AliceAutonomy.start()`, requiring **no additional Mint bridge marker** for the initial runtime install.
- Status questions are intercepted in the instance `handleMessage` method with no LLM call, no pathfinder or action cancellation. Reply includes state/action and matched command sender where possible; unknown attribution is stated explicitly.
- Chat queries optionally use a separate Ollama `/api/chat` model with 1–12 second bounded timeout (default 5s), maximum 2 concurrent requests, a recent command cancellation check, and sanitization that strips command syntax. If no model is configured, existing Mindcraft LLM remains responsible for chat.
- Idle Minecraft jokes/commentary are sparse and suppressed by actions, FOLLOW intent, nearby danger, no nearby player or recent chat.
- Added classification/provenance/runtime regression test cases and documentation. **Actual Mint integration and live multiplayer behavior have not yet been observed.** In particular, the external Mint-local fast router may have message-path differences; verify source attribution and chat delivery live.

## 2026-10-18 — Mint incident: dual survival owners / Ollama port / harvest interruption

User's uploaded `node main.js` run showed:
- Alice gathered logs, autonomously crafted planks and a workbench, but `wooden_sword` had `undefined` textual count and zero confirmed inventory delta. It was not proven to have been crafted or retained.
- `mode:self_preservation` repeatedly interrupted AliceSafety and failed at `src/agent/modes.js:82` with `skills.moveAway is not a function`. Alice had HP near 0 and was killed by zombies twice. Root cause is competing survival owners and a broken old skill dependency.
- `mode:aliceSafety` tried to stop `action:aliceCollectWood` and logged many `waiting for code to finish executing...` iterations and `MaxListenersExceededWarning` for `collectBlock_finished`. This suggests incomplete/slow cancellation; precise listener ownership still requires a Mint trace.
- Inventory reset on death yielded `[Alice gather] collected -1/10`; this was not actual negative collection.
- Ollama planner could not reach `127.0.0.1:11435` (ECONNREFUSED), so ordinary LLM-driven messages like "保护我" and "收集圆石" returned `No response data.`
- `跟着我` was incorrectly ignored as `already following` while `GATHER` was active; this is inside Mint's unversioned fast router and **is not fixed by these Alice runtime changes**.

Fixes staged in this build: block broken old survival mode in Core, request harvest-plugin cancellation before survival preemption, add low-health proximity/hysteresis, handle death, clamp collection progress, verify crafted inventory asynchronously, avoid wielding junk while chopping logs, and test each policy. The Mac deployment script can also establish a local-only reverse SSH Ollama tunnel when needed. No local Mint/MC tests have been run by the assistant; real restart and mob interaction are required.

Known remaining items: migrate and test actual Mint `agent.js`/fast-router, bounded cancellation of `collectBlock` if plugin hangs, real follow priority while critical HP, version-compatible `craftRecipe` behavior, food provisioning, and persistent player guard activation when 20B is unavailable.

- Elective AliceAutonomy jobs are now skipped while health is below 14/20 or the real-time safety controller sees a hostile, to avoid starting workbench/weapon crafting in active danger.

Exercise data minimization before sharing.

## 2026-10-18 — Standalone chest / delivery and crafting dependency primitives

- Added `runtime/alice_modules/storage.js`: bounded chest/barrel operations only when a caller explicitly supplies a visible block within 4.5 blocks; preserves weapons, food and an initial reserve of basic materials; verifies live inventory quantity changes and closes windows; supports withdrawing exact item counts and dropping items within 4 blocks of an actual visible player. Tossing verifies Alice's lost inventory count, **not the player's pickup**.
- Added `recipe_plan.js`: finite, deterministic recipe dependency expansion against a caller-provided version-specific recipe resolver; calculates missing base materials, workbench requirement, and intended crafting steps. No LLM actions, shell, background mining or automatic crafting execution. Tests use synthetic recipes, **not Paper recipes**.
- Four storage API mock tests and three recipe planner tests passed locally. These helpers are **not attached to the unversioned Mint fast command router or ActionManager**; commands like `放到箱子`, `搬家取箱子里的东西`, `做XX`, `给我XX` remain acceptance-pending, not live features.

- AliceSocial now acknowledges the receipt of recognized follow/wood/basic-weapon instructions in Chinese (including Mint raw chat), deduplicated for 2.5 seconds. Receipt is deliberately not an execution/success claim; Mint fast router and command admission remain unversioned.
- Recipe planner `materialsAvailable` means raw inventory ingredient coverage only, not placed-workbench availability or real-world craft success.

## 2026-10-18 — Fix Mac Bash 3.2 crash after dashboard nginx -t success

Actual user Mac output showed nginx config syntactically valid, Nginx started and both URLs printed, then `run-dashboard.sh: line 90: WEB_PORT?: unbound variable`. Inspection located line 90 `$WEB_PORT；` followed directly by a full-width Chinese semicolon and line 91 `$API_PORT。` followed by Chinese full stop under `set -u`. Braced both variable expansions as `${WEB_PORT}`/`${API_PORT}` to force an unambiguous lexical boundary in macOS Bash 3.2; no Nginx configuration, listener addresses, login policy, credentials or Minecraft runtime changed. Added specific static regression and Bash syntax check. Nginx may have been terminated by the shell's EXIT cleanup after the error; user should restart the dashboard and verify from Mac + LAN client. Host verification pending.

## 2026-10-18 — Live 17879 autonomy and combat state instability: source-tested fix (not Mint-verified)

New `bedrock-native/experiment/src/operations.cjs` gathers up to 36 visible log candidates and groups by x/z trunk columns, examines lower known logs first, requires observed vertical trunk (>=2), actual leaves (>=3), and >=26 locally loaded near-base samples, and refuses close build markers or unsupported world block data. It reports local-follow-position progress every ~6.5s with `source:mineflayer_local_estimate_not_server_confirmed`, so real movement must still be verified in-game. Idle weapon preparation with insufficient wood starts an explicit `GATHER` task, no misleading `CRAFT` label. Local Node22 tests: **23/23 pass** including 5 added regression tests for missing peripheral block data, protected build, near-base coverage, 4.5s safety hysteresis and no-material GATHER, plus attack truthfulness. Static syntax passes. **Not yet deployed/tested on user's Mint; do not claim fix is validated in the real world.** Keep the original verified backup gate on port 17879, online-mode=true and known-good chat-only fallback; avoid 17880 unless user requests it.
